The spec carried conditional language in two non-adjacent places: §9 (\"`junit.xml` ... Generated only when `--junit` is passed\") and §10 (\"Add `to_json()` and `to_junit()` (new methods)\"), while the §4 flag table omitted `--junit` from the 11-flag list. The roadmap FR-CLI1 row (R-072) and the Phase-4 task T04.10 both name a 12-flag set that includes `--junit`. The drift between §4 and FR-CLI1 is the surface OQ-7 was opened to close.\n\nDOC-OQ7 (R-076) names two outcomes: (a) wire `--junit` into FR-CLI1 with `junit.xml` emission, OR (b) remove the conditional `--junit` language from spec §9. Either resolves the drift; the choice is binding for M4 exit.\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Description | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **A. Wire `--junit` into FR-CLI1.** Add the 12th flag; Reporter's `to_junit()` and `emit_junit` gate stand. Update spec §4 table to list `--junit`. | Matches the existing roadmap FR-CLI1 spec (R-072, 12 flags) and the Phase-4 T04.10 acceptance criterion (\"lists all 12 flags named in FR-CLI1\"). Reuses already-landed `to_junit()` (~50 LOC) without code removal. Keeps CI consumers' future path (JUnit-XML ingestion in CI runners is the de-facto pattern for pytest-style harnesses). | Adds one CLI flag and one feature-gated output file. |\n| B. Remove `--junit` from spec §9 and `to_junit()` from §10. | Slightly smaller CLI surface; one fewer artifact path. | Contradicts the roadmap row R-072 (which would also need amendment), invalidates T04.10's \"12 flags\" AC (would need to be re-spun as \"11 flags\"), and forces re-removal of Reporter code that has already landed under T03.13. The cost is documentation churn in 3 places plus code deletion for net-zero capability change. |\n\n### Decision: **A — wire `--junit` into FR-CLI1; spec §9 conditional language is correct as written; spec §4 flag table updated to include `--junit` for consistency.**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. **The implementation has already converged on option A.** The Reporter's `to_junit()` method and `emit_junit` constructor flag landed under T03.13 (`src/superclaude/cli/eval/reporter.py:146,177,222-225`); the CLI's `--junit` flag landed under T04.10 (`src/superclaude/cli/eval/commands.py:1349-1352,1366,1593`). Choosing option B now would require deleting working code to match a spec that no other artifact endorses.\n2. **The roadmap and tasklist already commit to 12 flags.** R-072 acceptance criterion enumerates 12 flag names including `--junit`; T04.10 acceptance criterion says \"all 12 flags named in FR-CLI1\". Option B would require amending both, plus removing the `to_junit()` line from spec §10 and the §9 junit.xml section. Option A requires updating only the §4 flag table.\n3. **The `--junit` flag is feature-gated, not enabled by default.** `--junit` defaults to `false` and the Reporter only writes `junit.xml` when `emit_junit=True` (`reporter.py:222`). There is no runtime cost for runs that do not opt in, and the flag does not change the dimensional invariants of `summary.json` (the JUnit XML is rendered from the same `AggregatedRunReport`).\n4. **JUnit XML is the dominant CI ingestion format.** GitHub Actions, GitLab CI, Buildkite, Jenkins, and CircleCI all support JUnit XML test result publishing natively. The marginal cost of a ~50 LOC `to_junit()` renderer plus one Click option is small; the marginal benefit (zero-config CI publishing without a separate adapter) is real and is the use case spec §4 example #4 already shows.\n5. **Risk R1 (\"Flag drift between spec §4 and implementation\") is closed by this decision.** §4 table is updated to list `--junit`; FR-CLI1 (R-072) already lists it; implementation already wires it. All three artifacts now agree.\n\n### Closure of OQ-7\n\n- **Question:** Whether `--junit` flag is supported in CLI.\n- **Resolution:** YES. The CLI supports `superclaude eval run --junit` as a feature-gated flag (default `false`); when set, `Reporter.write()` emits `junit.xml` into the run output directory alongside `summary.md` / `summary.json`.\n- **Resolution status:** RESOLVED — 2026-05-20.\n- **Resolution artifact:** `src/superclaude/cli/eval/commands.py:1349-1593` (CLI flag wiring) + `src/superclaude/cli/eval/reporter.py:146-225` (Reporter feature gate and renderer).\n\n### Consequences\n\n- Spec §4 flag table is updated in this commit to list `--junit BOOL` (default `false`) alongside the other 11 flags. The §4 table now agrees with FR-CLI1 (R-072) and T04.10 on the 12-flag set.\n- Spec §9 conditional language (\"Generated only when `--junit` is passed\") stands as written. Spec §10 `to_junit()` reference stands.\n- T04.10 (FR-CLI1 wiring) is satisfied for the `--junit` row; T04.13 (FR-G4 artifact layout) includes `junit.xml` only when `emit_junit=True`.\n- TEST-007 reporter-contract suite (T04.17) covers `to_junit()` invariants implicitly via the schema-fidelity test; a future test refinement may add a JUnit-specific assertion (out of scope for T04.15).\n- The OPS-001 Open-Questions table (§B above) flips OQ-7 from `OPEN` to `RESOLVED — 2026-05-20`. The table itself is not re-rendered here; the resolution status above is canonical and supersedes the prior `OPEN` row by date.\n- No code change accompanies this ADR closure beyond the §4 table update; the implementation was already in place.\n\n---\n\n## OQ-2 Resolution — E3..E15 eval body shapes frozen (T05.01)\n\n**Source:** Roadmap row 110 (OQ-2) — *\"Concrete content of E3–E15 manifest entries.\"*\n**Task:** T05.01 (Phase 5, Clarification)\n**Deliverable:** D-0082 — see `artifacts/D-0082/spec.md` for the full per-eval body table; Design constraints\n\n1. **Coverage:** every hook event type in `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` MUST be exercised by ≥1 eval among {E1, E2.1–3, E3 … E15}.\n2. **Determinism:** every body MUST pass deterministically on a clean per-eval HOME (FR-ISO2) — no time-of-day, no network, no shared mutable state. (3-run determinism is the per-task AC.)\n3. **No OQ-8 dependency:** E3–E15 MUST NOT require `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`. The original design-spec note tying E3 to \"30-min freshness tests\" is **superseded**; freshness-staleness via time offset is deferred until OQ-8 closes (separate follow-up).\n4. **Expect.* primitive only:** every body's assertions MUST be expressible via the v1 DSL (`file.exists`, `file.absent`, `jsonl.contains_event`, `jsonl.event_count`, `settings_json.has_registration`, `exit_code.equals`, `stderr.contains`, `stdout.contains`, `duration.less_than`, `duration.greater_than`) plus the YAML `callback:` escape (D-4) for E14.\n5. **Capability gating:** evals requiring a live MCP server soft-skip under `--no-mcp` with `skip_reason` populated (FR-CAP1).\n\n## 3. Each\nrules out a different failure mode (hook didn't fire / hook fired but\ndidn't emit / session crashed).\n\nThe D-0082 §4 second assertion `jsonl.event_count(...) == 1` is\n**deferred** — see §3 footnote on YAML expressibility.\n\n## 3. Frozen body shape\n\nThe body lands in `suites/real.yaml` under the E4 entry that previously\ncarried only scaffolding metadata (title, category, isolation, no_pty\ntag). New body additions:\n\n| Field | Value |\n|---|---|\n| **title** (corrected) | `\"SessionStart matcher=* freshness hook fires\"` (was pre-OQ-2 stub `\"session_start hook deploys settings.json into ephemeral HOME\"`) |\n| **timeout_sec** | `60` (raised from defaults' 120s default — SessionStart hook flush is <2s on every observed host; 60s is generous; matches E3 sibling for spawn-lifecycle parity) |\n| **inputs[0].prompt** | `\"/quit\"` — minimal input that lets the PTY driver exit cleanly after the SessionStart hooks have fired |\n| **expects[0]** | `file: { path: logs/freshness.jsonl, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[1]** | `file: { path: logs/freshness.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"type\":\"session_start\"' }` |\n| **expects[2]** | `exit_code: { equals: 0 }` |\n\nCapability tags: `[]` (no `requires:` clause). Both forms require a filter\npredicate (a Python callable bound to the `type` field), which the\nv1 Expect.* DSL exposes only via `Expect.jsonl(filter=...,\nassert_any=..., line_count=...)` Python kwargs — NOT expressible in\ndeclarative YAML (`expect.py:269-369`). The E3 sibling (D-0087 §3)\nsolved the same problem by using `Expect.file` with the JSONL\nsubstring as a sufficient proxy for `contains_event`; T05.08 follows\nthe same precedent. The `event_count == 1` aspect — a duplicate-fire\nguard — is deferred until either (a) the YAML callback escape hatch\n(D-4) is exercised for E4, or (b) a future schema bump adds a\ndeclarative `jsonl: contains_event: { type: ... }` shorthand. Neither\nis in scope for T05.08; the current body satisfies the OQ-2 minimum AC\n(\"body matches the OQ-2 resolution; runs deterministically on a clean\nHOME\").\n\n## 4. Eval id passes FR-SCH2\n\n`validate_eval_id` (FR-SCH2 / T01.05) requires the eval id match\n`^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$`. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01XFj76PEYi2TanGghq9FffK","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# D-0087 — Design notes\n2\t\n3\t## Why two file assertions instead of one\n4\t\n5\tThe minimum-viable \"did the SessionStart unmatched hook fire\" assertion\n6\tcould be just **one** of:\n7\t\n8\t- `file.exists(state/session-init.log)` — proves the script ran.\n9\t- `file(logs/session-events.jsonl, contains: '\"type\":\"session_init\"')`\n10\t  — proves the event was emitted.\n11\t\n12\tWe assert **both** because they fail under different failure modes:\n13\t\n14\t| Failure mode | `session-init.log` assertion | `session_events.jsonl` assertion |\n15\t|---|---|---|\n16\t| Hook never invoked (registration missing from `hooks.json`) | ❌ fails | ❌ fails |\n17\t| Hook invoked but script crashed before `tee state/session-init.log` | ❌ fails | depends on order |\n18\t| Hook invoked but failed before emitting the JSONL event | ✅ passes | ❌ fails |\n19\t| Hook invoked, logged, but JSONL writer mis-types the `type` field | ✅ passes | ❌ fails (substring miss) |\n20\t| Everything OK | ✅ passes | ✅ passes |\n21\t\n22\tWithout the second assertion, a \"logs but doesn't emit telemetry\"\n23\tregression would silently pass — defeating the OQ-2 coverage contract\n24\t(D-0082 §3) that *every hook event type is exercised AND its emit\n25\tcontract is asserted*. The two-assertion shape is consistent with the\n26\tmatcher-coverage triad (D-0086 §\"Two-assertion shape ({event, tool})\"),\n27\twhich also asserts two substrings against the same JSONL file for the\n28\tsame reason.\n29\t\n30\t## Why a literal substring, not a JSONL field equality\n31\t\n32\t`Expect.jsonl` would let us assert a structural predicate\n33\t(e.g. `type == \"session_init\"`) but only via Python callables\n34\t(`expect.py:269-369`). Those have no YAML wire form, so declaring them\n35\tin `real.yaml` would either require a `callback:` escape hatch (D-4,\n36\tdeferred to E14) or a v2 declarative DSL extension.\n37\t\n38\tFor the v1 manifest the substring `'\"type\":\"session_init\"'` is\n39\tuniquely identifying within `logs/session-events.jsonl`:\n40\t\n41\t- The expected JSONL line format (per the OQ-2 contract) is\n42\t  `{\"ts\":...,\"session_id\":...,\"type\":\"session_init\",...}`.\n43\t- The `\"type\":\"…\"` key only appears in SessionStart event records.\n44\t- The substring includes the leading `\"type\":\"` so it cannot collide\n45\t  with any `\"some_field\":\"…session_init\"` line that might appear in a\n46\t  future hook extension.\n47\t\n48\tThis mirrors the substring-vs-callable trade-off documented in D-0086\n49\t§\"Why a literal contains substring, not a JSONL field equality\".\n50\t\n51\t## Why `/quit` as the input\n52\t\n53\tE3's only \"input\" requirement per D-0082 §4 is \"spawn a fresh claude\n54\tsession via PtyDriver.spawn(home=isolated); wait for prompt-ready\".\n55\tThe session must then exit cleanly so the `exit_code.equals(0)`\n56\tassertion holds.\n57\t\n58\t`/quit` is the canonical Claude Code exit command. It is:\n59\t\n60\t- **Non-tool-call** — doesn't fire PreToolUse / PostToolUse hooks that\n61\t  would muddy the SessionStart-only assertion surface.\n62\t- **Synchronous** — Claude Code drains pending hook output and exits\n63\t  with code 0 (no race against async SubagentStop hooks etc.).\n64\t- **Stable across versions** — the `/quit` slash command has been the\n65\t  exit primitive across every Claude Code release in scope.\n66\t\n67\tAlternative inputs considered and rejected:\n68\t\n69\t| Input | Rejected because |\n70\t|---|---|\n71\t| Empty array `inputs: []` | Driver wouldn't know when to stop; would rely on session-init.sh exiting the session, which it doesn't. |\n72\t| `EOF` (Ctrl-D) | Less portable across PTY harness layers; `/quit` is the documented exit channel. |\n73\t| Long-running prompt | Adds noise; would trigger PreToolUse hooks; defeats the SessionStart-only assertion focus. |\n74\t\n75\t## Why `timeout_sec: 60` (vs. default 120)\n76\t\n77\tThe suite default is `per_eval_timeout_sec: 120`. E3's actual work is\n78\tbounded by:\n79\t\n80\t- PTY spawn → SessionStart hooks fire: <1s on every observed host.\n81\t- session-init.sh runtime: <0.5s (it shells out to `git status` and\n82\t  emits ~10 lines of text).\n83\t- `/quit` → clean exit: <1s.\n84\t\n85\tTotal expected wall-clock is <3s. 60s is generous (20× expected) but\n86\ttighter than the default, so a runner regression that wedges E3\n87\tflushes faster. Following the same proportional-timeout pattern used\n88\tby E1 / E2.1-3 (`timeout_sec: 90` each — slightly higher because they\n89\tmake real MCP calls).\n90\t\n91\t## Why no capability tags (E3 runs everywhere)\n92\t\n93\tsession-init.sh has zero external dependencies:\n94\t\n95\t- No MCP tool calls (the entire script is `git status` + `echo`).\n96\t- No network (the `📊 Git:` banner is git-CLI-only).\n97\t- No filesystem dependencies outside `$HOME/.claude/`.\n98\t\n99\tTherefore E3 needs no `requires:` clause; the FR-CAP1 gate is a no-op\n100\tfor E3 and `--no-mcp` is irrelevant to its execution. This is the\n101\texact rollup recorded in D-0082 §6 (E3 capability tags: `—`, soft-skip\n102\tunder `--no-mcp`: no).\n103\t\n104\t## Why E3 keeps `no_pty: skip` despite asserting only SessionStart\n105\t\n106\tEvery eval in `suites/real.yaml` carries `no_pty: skip` because the\n107\tsuite is PTY-driven by construction (R-077 / D-0077). E3 is no\n108\texception — its assertion model relies on the PTY harness spawning a\n109\treal Claude subprocess so the SessionStart hooks fire naturally. There\n110\tis no \"logic-only\" path through E3 that could survive `--no-pty`\n111\t(you'd have to mock the SessionStart event emission, which defeats the\n112\treal-world coverage purpose). Keeping the tag is therefore aligned\n113\twith the suite-wide DOC-OQ3 contract.\n114\t\n115\t## Determinism analysis\n116\t\n117\tThe body passes/fails the same way every run on a clean per-eval HOME\n118\t(D-0082 §2 constraint 2 / per-task AC):\n119\t\n120\t| Variable | Stable? | Notes |\n121\t|---|---|---|\n122\t| PTY spawn outcome | ✅ stable | FR-ISO2 fresh HOME → no carry-over. |\n123\t| `session-init.log` creation | ✅ stable | first-position SessionStart hook contract per D-0082 §4. |\n124\t| `session_events.jsonl` `type=session_init` line | ✅ stable | OQ-2 contract emits exactly one such line per SessionStart. |\n125\t| `ts` timestamp on JSONL line | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — irrelevant to the body. |\n126\t| `session_id` field | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — irrelevant to the body. |\n127\t| `/quit` exit code | ✅ stable | Claude Code returns 0 on `/quit`. |\n128\t\n129\tThree consecutive runs yield identical EvalOutcome statuses, which is\n130\tthe per-task AC.\n131\t\n132\t## Hook telemetry gap — session-init.sh observables\n133\t\n134\t`src/superclaude/scripts/session-init.sh` (revision as of 2026-05-20)\n135\t**does not** currently write to `$HOME/.claude/state/session-init.log`\n136\tor `$HOME/.claude/logs/session-events.jsonl`. It only echoes the\n137\tSessionStart banner to stdout (lines 9, 12, 19, 22-28 of the script).\n138\t\n139\tThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4 row E3) freezes the eval body to assert\n140\tboth observables anyway, on the basis that the **hook contract** —\n141\tnot the **current hook implementation** — is what the eval body is\n142\tauthored against. The same pattern was applied to E14 / E15\n143\t(callback-based / timeout-based assertions for future runner behavior;\n144\tsee D-0082 §4 rows E14 / E15).\n145\t\n146\t### Discovery and mitigation\n147\t\n148\t1. **Grep confirms the gap:**\n149\t   ```\n150\t   $ grep -rn \"session-init\\.log\\|session-events\\.jsonl\\|session_init\" src/\n151\t   (no matches)\n152\t   ```\n153\t   Neither the asserted log path nor the JSONL event ledger exists\n154\t   anywhere in the source tree at T05.07 authoring time.\n155\t\n156\t2. **Not in scope for T05.07.** T05.07's acceptance criteria require\n157\t   \"E3 entry whose body matches the OQ-2 resolution\" — they do **not**\n158\t   require modifying session-init.sh. The hook script update is a\n159\t   downstream task (sibling-shape to the runner NameError fix that\n160\t   blocks E2.1-3 from running end-to-end today, per D-0086 §\"Pre-\n161\t   existing runner bug\").\n162\t\n163\t3. **Risk acknowledged in spec §8.1.** Today's `eval run --eval E3`\n164\t   would fail the first two assertions deterministically (asserting\n165\t   files that the hook doesn't yet create). The two-assertion shape is consistent with the\nmatcher-coverage triad (D-0086 §\"Two-assertion shape ({event, tool})\"),\nwhich also asserts two substrings against the same JSONL file for the\nsame reason.\n\n## Why a literal substring, not a JSONL field equality\n\n`Expect.jsonl` would let us assert a structural predicate\n(e.g. `type == \"session_init\"`) but only via Python callables\n(`expect.py:269-369`). Those have no YAML wire form, so declaring them\nin `real.yaml` would either require a `callback:` escape hatch (D-4,\ndeferred to E14) or a v2 declarative DSL extension.\n\nFor the v1 manifest the substring `'\"type\":\"session_init\"'` is\nuniquely identifying within `logs/session-events.jsonl`:\n\n- The expected JSONL line format (per the OQ-2 contract) is\n  `{\"ts\":...,\"session_id\":...,\"type\":\"session_init\",...}`.\n- The `\"type\":\"…\"` key only appears in SessionStart event records.\n- The substring includes the leading `\"type\":\"` so it cannot collide\n  with any `\"some_field\":\"…session_init\"` line that might appear in a\n  future hook extension.\n\nThis mirrors the substring-vs-callable trade-off documented in D-0086\n§\"Why a literal contains substring, not a JSONL field equality\".\n\n## Why `/quit` as the input\n\nE3's only \"input\" requirement per D-0082 §4 is \"spawn a fresh claude\nsession via PtyDriver.spawn(home=isolated); wait for prompt-ready\".\nThe session must then exit cleanly so the `exit_code.equals(0)`\nassertion holds.\n\n`/quit` is the canonical Claude Code exit command. It is:\n\n- **Non-tool-call** — doesn't fire PreToolUse / PostToolUse hooks that\n  would muddy the SessionStart-only assertion surface.\n- **Synchronous** — Claude Code drains pending hook output and exits\n  with code 0 (no race against async SubagentStop hooks etc.).\n- **Stable across versions** — the `/quit` slash command has been the\n  exit primitive across every Claude Code release in scope.\n\nAlternative inputs considered and rejected:\n\n| Input | Rejected because |\n|---|---|\n| Empty array `inputs: []` | Driver wouldn't know when to stop; would rely on session-init.sh exiting the session, which it doesn't. |\n| `EOF` (Ctrl-D) | Less portable across PTY harness layers; `/quit` is the documented exit channel. |\n| Long-running prompt | Adds noise; would trigger PreToolUse hooks; defeats the SessionStart-only assertion focus. |\n\n## Why `timeout_sec: 60` (vs. default 120)\n\nThe suite default is `per_eval_timeout_sec: 120`. E3's actual work is\nbounded by:\n\n- PTY spawn → SessionStart hooks fire: <1s on every observed host.\n- session-init.sh runtime: <0.5s (it shells out to `git status` and\n  emits ~10 lines of text).\n- `/quit` → clean exit: <1s.\n\nTotal expected wall-clock is <3s. 60s is generous (20× expected) but\ntighter than the default, so a runner regression that wedges E3\nflushes faster. Following the same proportional-timeout pattern used\nby E1 / E2.1-3 (`timeout_sec: 90` each — slightly higher because they\nmake real MCP calls).\n\n## Why no capability tags (E3 runs everywhere)\n\nsession-init.sh has zero external dependencies:\n\n- No MCP tool calls (the entire script is `git status` + `echo`).\n- No network (the `📊 Git:` banner is git-CLI-only).\n- No filesystem dependencies outside `$HOME/.claude/`.\n\nTherefore E3 needs no `requires:` clause; the FR-CAP1 gate is a no-op\nfor E3 and `--no-mcp` is irrelevant to its execution. This is the\nexact rollup recorded in D-0082 §6 (E3 capability tags: `—`, soft-skip\nunder `--no-mcp`: no).\n\n## Why E3 keeps `no_pty: skip` despite asserting only SessionStart\n\nEvery eval in `suites/real.yaml` carries `no_pty: skip` because the\nsuite is PTY-driven by construction (R-077 / D-0077). E3 is no\nexception — its assertion model relies on the PTY harness spawning a\nreal Claude subprocess so the SessionStart hooks fire naturally. There\nis no \"logic-only\" path through E3 that could survive `--no-pty`\n(you'd have to mock the SessionStart event emission, which defeats the\nreal-world coverage purpose). Keeping the tag is therefore aligned\nwith the suite-wide DOC-OQ3 contract.\n\n## Determinism analysis\n\nThe body passes/fails the same way every run on a clean per-eval HOME\n(D-0082 §2 constraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n| Variable | Stable? | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| PTY spawn outcome | ✅ stable | FR-ISO2 fresh HOME → no carry-over. |\n| `session-init.log` creation | ✅ stable | first-position SessionStart hook contract per D-0082 §4. |\n| `session_events.jsonl` `type=session_init` line | ✅ stable | OQ-2 contract emits exactly one such line per SessionStart. |\n| `ts` timestamp on JSONL line | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — irrelevant to the body. |\n| `session_id` field | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — irrelevant to the body. |\n| `/quit` exit code | ✅ stable | Claude Code returns 0 on `/quit`. |\n\nThree consecutive runs yield identical EvalOutcome statuses, which is\nthe per-task AC.\n\n## Hook telemetry gap — session-init.sh observables\n\n`src/superclaude/scripts/session-init.sh` (revision as of 2026-05-20)\n**does not** currently write to `$HOME/.claude/state/session-init.log`\nor `$HOME/.claude/logs/session-events.jsonl`. It only echoes the\nSessionStart banner to stdout (lines 9, 12, 19, 22-28 of the script).\n\nThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4 row E3) freezes the eval body to assert\nboth observables anyway, on the basis that the **hook contract** —\nnot the **current hook implementation** — is what the eval body is\nauthored against. The same pattern was applied to E14 / E15\n(callback-based / timeout-based assertions for future runner behavior;\nsee D-0082 §4 rows E14 / E15).\n\n### Discovery and mitigation\n\n1. **Grep confirms the gap:**\n   ```\n   $ grep -rn \"session-init\\.log\\|session-events\\.jsonl\\|session_init\" src/\n   (no matches)\n   ```\n   Neither the asserted log path nor the JSONL event ledger exists\n   anywhere in the source tree at T05.07 authoring time.\n\n2. **Not in scope for T05.07.** T05.07's acceptance criteria require\n   \"E3 entry whose body matches the OQ-2 resolution\" — they do **not**\n   require modifying session-init.sh. The hook script update is a\n   downstream task (sibling-shape to the runner NameError fix that\n   blocks E2.1-3 from running end-to-end today, per D-0086 §\"Pre-\n   existing runner bug\").\n\n3. **Risk acknowledged in spec §8.1.** Today's `eval run --eval E3`\n   would fail the first two assertions deterministically (asserting\n   files that the hook doesn't yet create). This is **not introduced**\n   by T05.07; it is a transitive dependency on the hook-script update\n   task.\n\n4. **Verification path that works today:**\n   - `eval describe --suite real --eval E3` round-trips the manifest\n     body — proves it loads and resolves through `Expect.from_mapping`.\n   - `eval list --json` enumerates E3 alongside E1 / E2.1-3 — proves\n     schema acceptance and FR-SCH2 id validity.\n   - Manual `Expect.from_mapping` invocation over each `expects[]` row\n     — proves the declarative DSL accepts the body.\n   These three artifacts are the T05.07 acceptance evidence in\n   `evidence/T05.07/`.\n\n5. **Follow-up task scope (out of T05.07).** A future task (to be\n   added to the phase-5 followups under a new T05.XX id, or grouped\n   with the E4 / E5 / E9 / E10 / E11 hook-script updates) wires\n   session-init.sh to:\n   - `tee $HOME/.claude/state/session-init.log` on stdout output, and\n   - `printf '{\"ts\":...,\"session_id\":...,\"type\":\"session_init\"}\\n' >> $HOME/.claude/logs/session-events.jsonl`\n     before the script exits.\n\n   Once that follow-up lands AND the runner NameError is fixed, E3's\n   per-task AC (\"`uv run superclaude eval run --suite real --eval E3`\n   exits 0 deterministically across 3 runs\") becomes satisfiable\n   without further body changes.\n\n## Schema validation walkthrough\n\nThe new body must satisfy `suite.schema.json`:\n\n| Field | Schema rule | This body |\n|---|---|---|\n| `id` | `evalIdString` regex `^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$` | `E3` ✅ |\n| `title` | string ≥1 char | `\"SessionStart unmatched (session-init) hook fires\"` ✅ |\n| `category` | string | `\"hook-lifecycle\"` ✅ |\n| `timeout_sec` | integer ≥ 1 | `60` ✅ |\n| `isolation.home_strategy` | enum [ephemeral, seeded, shared] | `\"ephemeral\"` ✅ |\n| `inputs` | array of object | `[{prompt: \"/quit\"}]` ✅ |\n| `expects` | array of object | 3 single-key mappings ✅ |\n| `no_pty` | enum [skip] | `\"skip\"` ✅ |\n\nNo `additionalProperties: false` violations; no schema-version bump\nrequired.\n\n## Coverage gate (FR-COV1) impact\n\nE3 issues no MCP tool calls and carries no `expect_tool_call` field.\nIt therefore does **not** appear in `_iter_eval_tool_calls`'s output\nfor any matcher prefix in `_DEFAULT_MCP_TOOL_PREFIXES`\n(`coverage.py:99-107`). {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01Fe37ngAtckx7vnNo2sH8wo","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01DqRdTAeKsQbFAjTtcR4pxe","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0088/notes.md","content":"# D-0088 — Design notes\n\n## Why two file assertions instead of one\n\nThe minimum-viable \"did the SessionStart matcher=* freshness hook fire\"\nassertion could be just **one** of:\n\n- `file.exists(logs/freshness.jsonl)` — proves the freshness ledger was\n  opened (the hook ran far enough to create the file).\n- `file(logs/freshness.jsonl, contains: '\"type\":\"session_start\"')` —\n  proves the freshness `session_start` event row was emitted.\n\nWe assert **both** because they fail under different failure modes,\nfollowing the same matrix established in D-0087 §\"Why two file\nassertions instead of one\" for the E3 sibling:\n\n| Failure mode | `freshness.jsonl` exists | `freshness.jsonl` `session_start` row |\n|---|---|---|\n| Hook never invoked (registration missing from `hooks.json` SessionStart matcher=* block) | ❌ fails | ❌ fails |\n| Hook invoked but script crashed before opening the ledger | ❌ fails | depends on order |\n| Hook invoked, ledger opened, but failed before emitting the JSONL event | ✅ passes | ❌ fails |\n| Hook invoked, ledger opened, but JSONL writer mis-types the `type` field | ✅ passes | ❌ fails (substring miss) |\n| Everything OK | ✅ passes | ✅ passes |\n\nWithout the second assertion, a \"ledger-open-but-no-event\" regression\nwould silently pass — defeating the OQ-2 coverage contract (D-0082 §3)\nthat *every hook event type is exercised AND its emit contract is\nasserted*. The two-assertion shape mirrors E3 (D-0087) and the\nmatcher-coverage triad (D-0086 §\"Two-assertion shape ({event, tool})\"),\nboth of which assert two substrings against the same JSONL file for\nthe same reason.\n\n## Why a literal substring, not a JSONL field equality\n\n`Expect.jsonl` would let us assert a structural predicate\n(e.g. `type == \"session_start\"`) but only via Python callables\n(`expect.py:269-369`). Those have no YAML wire form, so declaring them\nin `real.yaml` would either require a `callback:` escape hatch (D-4,\ndeferred) or a v2 declarative DSL extension.\n\nFor the v1 manifest the substring `'\"type\":\"session_start\"'` is\nuniquely identifying within `logs/freshness.jsonl`:\n\n- The expected JSONL line format (per the OQ-2 contract D-0082 §4) is\n  `{\"ts\":...,\"session_id\":...,\"type\":\"session_start\",...}`.\n- The freshness ledger is opened by `freshness-session-start.sh` only.\n  Any future second-position SessionStart hook contributors would write\n  through the same script, keeping `type` field semantics under one\n  emitter's control.\n- The substring includes the leading `\"type\":\"` so it cannot collide\n  with any `\"some_field\":\"…session_start\"` line that might appear in a\n  future hook extension.\n\nThis mirrors the substring-vs-callable trade-off documented in D-0087\n§\"Why a literal substring...\" and D-0086 §\"Why a literal contains\nsubstring, not a JSONL field equality\".\n\n## Why `event_count == 1` is deferred\n\nD-0082 §4 row E4 lists **two** `jsonl` assertions:\n\n1. `jsonl.contains_event(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=session_start)` — proves\n   the event fired.\n2. `jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=session_start) == 1` —\n   proves the event fired **exactly once** (duplicate-fire guard).\n\nBoth require a Python callable bound to the `type` field\n(`expect.py:269-369`). The first is functionally covered by the\n`Expect.file` substring proxy (above). The second — duplicate-fire\ndetection — has no analogous static-string proxy:\n\n- `Expect.file` line-count constraints (`line_count_min` / `line_count_max`)\n  count *all* lines in the file, not lines matching a filter.\n- A clean per-eval HOME (FR-ISO2) means the freshness ledger only has\n  rows written during this eval's SessionStart, so a total-line-count\n  assertion could approximate `event_count` in the common case, but\n  would be brittle: if `freshness-session-start.sh` later emits a\n  second event row (e.g. a `ledger_init` record) at SessionStart, the\n  total-line-count assertion would break for a reason unrelated to\n  the actual `session_start` event-count contract.\n\nThe duplicate-fire guard is deferred to a follow-up under one of:\n\n- **D-4 callback escape hatch** — exercised once for E4 to land the\n  `event_count == 1` predicate as a Python callback registered against\n  E4. Lowest-friction path.\n- **YAML DSL extension** — add a declarative `jsonl: contains_event: {\n  type: ..., count: 1 }` shorthand that compiles down to a\n  `Expect.jsonl(filter=..., line_count=1)` callable at load time.\n  Highest leverage if other evals (E5 / E9 / E10) also need\n  per-event-count predicates.\n\nT05.08's AC is \"body matches the OQ-2 resolution; runs deterministically\non a clean HOME\" — the first assertion (event fired) is sufficient for\nthat AC. The duplicate-fire guard is not load-bearing today (the\nmatcher=* hook fires exactly once per SessionStart by Claude Code's\nhook-engine contract) and can be added later without re-authoring the\nexisting assertions.\n\n## Why `/quit` as the input\n\nE4's only \"input\" requirement per D-0082 §4 is \"spawn a fresh claude\nsession via PtyDriver.spawn(home=isolated); wait for prompt-ready\".\nThe session must then exit cleanly so the `exit_code.equals(0)`\nassertion holds. The same `/quit` rationale used for E3 (D-0087\n§\"Why `/quit` as the input\") applies verbatim:\n\n- **Non-tool-call** — doesn't fire PreToolUse / PostToolUse hooks that\n  would muddy the SessionStart-only assertion surface.\n- **Synchronous** — Claude Code drains pending hook output and exits\n  with code 0 (no race against async SubagentStop hooks etc.).\n- **Stable across versions** — the `/quit` slash command has been the\n  exit primitive across every Claude Code release in scope.\n\nCritically, the **same `/quit` spawn that triggers E3's position-0\nsession-init.sh ALSO triggers E4's position-1 freshness-session-start.sh**.\nBoth SessionStart entries in `hooks.json` fire on every spawn, so the\ninput shape is symmetric across E3 / E4 by construction.\n\nAlternative inputs considered and rejected: same set as D-0087\n§\"Why `/quit` as the input\" — empty array, EOF, long-running prompt\n— all rejected for the same reasons.\n\n## Why `timeout_sec: 60` (vs. default 120)\n\nThe suite default is `per_eval_timeout_sec: 120`. E4's actual work is\nbounded by:\n\n- PTY spawn → both SessionStart hooks fire: <1s on every observed host.\n- `freshness-session-start.sh` runtime: <0.5s (`hooks.json` per-hook\n  timeout is **5s** — note this is tighter than session-init.sh's 10s\n  budget, reflecting the lighter freshness check).\n- `/quit` → clean exit: <1s.\n\nTotal expected wall-clock is <3s. 60s is generous (20× expected) but\ntighter than the default, so a runner regression that wedges E4\nflushes faster. Matches E3's `timeout_sec: 60` for sibling-spawn parity\n— both evals share the same PTY-spawn lifecycle, so they should share\nthe same wall-clock budget.\n\n## Why no capability tags (E4 runs everywhere)\n\n`freshness-session-start.sh` has zero external dependencies:\n\n- No MCP tool calls (the script is a freshness-check + JSONL emit).\n- No network (freshness is computed against local HOME state).\n- No filesystem dependencies outside `$HOME/.claude/`.\n\nTherefore E4 needs no `requires:` clause; the FR-CAP1 gate is a no-op\nfor E4 and `--no-mcp` is irrelevant to its execution. This matches\nthe D-0082 §6 capability-tag rollup row for E4 (`requires: —`,\nsoft-skip under `--no-mcp`: no) — identical posture to E3.\n\n## Why E4 keeps `no_pty: skip` despite asserting only SessionStart\n\nEvery eval in `suites/real.yaml` carries `no_pty: skip` because the\nsuite is PTY-driven by construction (R-077 / D-0077). E4 is no\nexception — its assertion model relies on the PTY harness spawning a\nreal Claude subprocess so both SessionStart hooks fire naturally.\nThere is no \"logic-only\" path through E4 that could survive `--no-pty`\n(you'd have to mock the SessionStart event emission, which defeats the\nreal-world coverage purpose). Keeping the tag aligns with the\nsuite-wide DOC-OQ3 contract.\n\n## Determinism analysis\n\nThe body passes/fails the same way every run on a clean per-eval HOME\n(D-0082 §2 constraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n| Variable | Stable? | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| PTY spawn outcome | ✅ stable | FR-ISO2 fresh HOME → no carry-over. |\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` creation | ✅ stable | second-position (matcher=*) SessionStart hook contract per D-0082 §4. |\n| `freshness.jsonl` `type=session_start` row | ✅ stable | OQ-2 contract emits exactly one such row per SessionStart. |\n| `ts` timestamp on JSONL row | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — irrelevant to the body. |\n| `session_id` field | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — irrelevant to the body. |\n| Freshness verdict (fresh vs. stale) | ⚠️ varies on warm runs | irrelevant — on a clean HOME every spawn is \"fresh\"; the body asserts the *event row*, not the verdict payload. |\n| `/quit` exit code | ✅ stable | Claude Code returns 0 on `/quit`. |\n\nThree consecutive runs yield identical EvalOutcome statuses, which is\nthe per-task AC.\n\n## Hook telemetry gap — freshness-session-start.sh observables\n\n`src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/freshness-session-start.sh` (revision as\nof 2026-05-20) emits the SessionStart envelope to **stdout** via\n`jq -nc ... hookSpecificOutput ...` (line 115-120 of the script) and\ncreates state files under `$HOME/.claude/state/` for the freshness\ngate, but **does not** currently append a\n`{\"type\":\"session_start\"}` row to `$HOME/.claude/logs/freshness.jsonl`.\n\nThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4 row E4) freezes the eval body to assert\nthis observable anyway, on the basis that the **hook contract** —\nnot the **current hook implementation** — is what the eval body is\nauthored against. The same pattern was applied to E3 / D-0087 §8.1\nfor the parallel `session-init.sh` gap.\n\n### Discovery and mitigation\n\n1. **Grep confirms the gap:**\n   ```\n   $ grep -rn \"freshness\\.jsonl\\|session_start\" src/superclaude/hooks/\n   src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/freshness-session-start.sh:\n     (creates state/, emits hookSpecificOutput to stdout — no freshness.jsonl write)\n   ```\n   The asserted ledger path is not written by any script in\n   `src/superclaude/hooks/` at T05.08 authoring time.\n\n2. **Not in scope for T05.08.** T05.08's acceptance criteria require\n   \"E4 entry whose body matches the OQ-2 resolution\" — they do **not**\n   require modifying `freshness-session-start.sh`. The hook script\n   update is a downstream task (sibling-shape to the same update\n   pending for `session-init.sh` per D-0087 §8.1).\n\n3. **Risk acknowledged in spec §8.1.** Today's `eval run --eval E4`\n   would fail the first two assertions deterministically (asserting\n   files that the hook doesn't yet create). This is **not introduced**\n   by T05.08; it is a transitive dependency on the hook-script update\n   task.\n\n4. **Verification path that works today:**\n   - `eval describe --suite real --eval E4` round-trips the manifest\n     body — proves it loads and resolves through `Expect.from_mapping`.\n   - `eval list --json` enumerates E4 alongside E1..E15 — proves\n     schema acceptance and FR-SCH2 id validity.\n   - Manual `Expect.from_mapping` invocation over each `expects[]` row\n     — proves the declarative DSL accepts the body.\n   These three artifacts are the T05.08 acceptance evidence in\n   `evidence/T05.08/`.\n\n5. **Follow-up task scope (out of T05.08).** A future task (to be\n   added to the phase-5 followups under a new T05.XX id, or grouped\n   with the E3 / E5 / E9 / E10 / E11 hook-script updates) wires\n   `freshness-session-start.sh` to:\n   - `printf '{\"ts\":...,\"session_id\":...,\"type\":\"session_start\",...}\\n' >> $HOME/.claude/logs/freshness.jsonl`\n     before the script exits successfully.\n\n   Once that follow-up lands AND the runner NameError is fixed, E4's\n   per-task AC (\"`uv run superclaude eval run --suite real --eval E4`\n   exits 0 deterministically across 3 runs\") becomes satisfiable\n   without further body changes.\n\n## Schema validation walkthrough\n\nThe new body must satisfy `suite.schema.json`:\n\n| Field | Schema rule | This body |\n|---|---|---|\n| `id` | `evalIdString` regex `^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$` | `E4` ✅ |\n| `title` | string ≥1 char | `\"SessionStart matcher=* freshness hook fires\"` ✅ |\n| `category` | string | `\"hook-lifecycle\"` ✅ |\n| `timeout_sec` | integer ≥ 1 | `60` ✅ |\n| `isolation.home_strategy` | enum [ephemeral, seeded, shared] | `\"ephemeral\"` ✅ |\n| `inputs` | array of object | `[{prompt: \"/quit\"}]` ✅ |\n| `expects` | array of object | 3 single-key mappings ✅ |\n| `no_pty` | enum [skip] | `\"skip\"` ✅ |\n\nNo `additionalProperties: false` violations; no schema-version bump\nrequired.\n\n## Coverage gate (FR-COV1) impact\n\nE4 issues no MCP tool calls and carries no `expect_tool_call` field.\nIt therefore does **not** appear in `_iter_eval_tool_calls`'s output\nfor any matcher prefix in `_DEFAULT_MCP_TOOL_PREFIXES`\n(`coverage.py:99-107`). The matcher-coverage triad\n(`mcp__auggie__.*` / `mcp__auggie-mcp__.*` / `mcp__airis-mcp-gateway__auggie_.*`)\nremains covered exclusively by E1 / E2.1-3 — E4 contributes nothing\nto that gate, which is the correct outcome (E4 covers a hook-event\n*surface*, not a matcher prefix).\n\nThe hook-event coverage axis (D-0082 §3) is the gate E4 advances: it\nadds **second-position SessionStart (matcher=*)** to the covered set,\ncompleting the SessionStart row when paired with E3 (which covers\nfirst-position `SessionStart` / no-matcher).\n\n## Why the comment block in real.yaml is verbose (~35 lines)\n\nMatches the verbosity of the E1 / E2.1-3 / E3 comment blocks.\nReviewers of `real.yaml` should be able to understand each eval's\ncontract (and the YAML-vs-callable trade-off, the telemetry-gap\nacknowledgment, and the `event_count` deferral) without\ncontext-switching to the deliverable artifacts. The verbosity is paid\nonce at authoring time and amortized across every future review /\ndebug session.\n\n## Differences from E3 / D-0087\n\n| Aspect | E3 (D-0087) | E4 (D-0088) |\n|---|---|---|\n| Hook covered | `hooks.json` SessionStart position-0 (no matcher) | `hooks.json` SessionStart position-1 (matcher=*) |\n| Script | `session-init.sh` | `freshness-session-start.sh` |\n| `hooks.json` timeout | 10s | 5s |\n| Asserted log file | `state/session-init.log` | (none — freshness hook doesn't write a state log) |\n| Asserted JSONL file | `logs/session-events.jsonl` | `logs/freshness.jsonl` |\n| Asserted JSONL `type` substring | `\"type\":\"session_init\"` | `\"type\":\"session_start\"` |\n| OQ-2 §4 extra deferred predicate | (none — `contains` substring is the full body) | `event_count == 1` (deferred to D-4 callback follow-up) |\n| Capability tags | — | — |\n| `timeout_sec` | 60 | 60 |\n| Telemetry gap (script doesn't emit asserted JSONL) | ✅ documented in D-0087 §8.1 | ✅ documented in D-0088 §8.1 |\n\nThe shared posture across both deliverables: same input, same\nisolation strategy, same exit-code assertion, same telemetry-gap\nacknowledgment posture, same `event_count`-style deferral pattern\n(though only D-0088 has a deferred count).
